]
*Section 3 for the explanation of differences between the two definitions). Our discussion of homotopy theory of semisimplicial objects can be phrased both in terms of this weak model structure or right semi-model structure. However, we prefer to provide more elementary arguments to make this section more self-contained.

12.10
.

If
\mathcal{E}
has finite coproducts, then the forgetful functor
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}\to{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.91666pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{
{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}
has a left adjoint.
It is given by

(LX)_{n}=\coprod_{[n]\rightarrowtriangle[m]}X_{m}

where the coproduct is over all degeneracy operators
[n]\rightarrowtriangle[m]
in
\Delta
.

Proof.

The functor
L
is the left Kan extension along
\Delta_{+}\to\Delta
.
If it can be computed pointwise, it is given by the formula

(LX)_{n}=\operatorname*{colim}_{[n]\to[m]}X_{m}

where the colimit is taken over the comma category
[n]\mathbin{\downarrow}\Delta_{+}^{\mathord{\mathrm{op}}}
.
(Its objects are arbitrary simplicial operators
[n]\to[m]
, but its morphisms are just the face operators.)
It follows from the existence of the degeneracy/face unique factorisation system in
\Delta
that
the discrete category of degeneracy operators
[n]\rightarrowtriangle[m]
is cofinal in this category.
Hence the colimit above can be rewritten as the the coproduct in the statement of the proposition.
Thus if
\mathcal{E}
has finite coproducts, this colimit exists which concludes the proof.
∎

12.11
.

The free functor
L\colon{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.91666pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{
{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}\to\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
preserves cofibrations and trivial cofibrations.

Proof.

It can be checked easily that the natural transformation from the initial functor to
L
satisfies the assumptions of
Lemma

3.20
, so it is enough to verify that
L
sends the generating cofibrations and trivial cofibrations to cofibrations and trivial cofibrations, respectively. These generators are of the form
\underline{\mathord{\Lambda}_{+}^{k}[n]}\rightarrowtail\underline{\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}
or
\underline{\mathord{\partial\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}}\rightarrowtail\underline{\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}
the image by
L
is computed as in
\mathsf{Set}
, thus giving
\underline{\mathord{\Lambda}^{k}[n]}\rightarrowtail\underline{\mathord{\Delta}[n]}
or
\underline{\mathord{\partial\mathord{\Delta}[n]}}\rightarrowtail\underline{\mathord{\Delta}[n]}
, i.e., the generating cofibrations and trivial cofibrations in
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
.
∎

12.12
.

The forgetful functor
U\colon\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}\to{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.91666pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{
{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}
preserves cofibrations and trivial cofibrations.

Proof.

The forgetful functor preserves all colimits that exist so it is enough to show that
the generating (trivial) cofibrations of
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
are sent to (trivial) cofibrations.
The case of cofibrations follows from
Theorems

4.6
and
12.3
.
For trivial cofibrations, note that if
X\in\mathsf{sSet}
, then
\underline{UX}=U\underline{X}

(the first
U
is the forgetful functor
\mathsf{sSet}\to{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

, the unit
X\to ULX
is a trivial cofibration.

Proof.

The composite
UL
preserves all the relevant colimits, so it is enough to check that
for each generating cofibration
\underline{\mathord{\partial\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}}\to\underline{\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}
, the map

UL(\underline{\mathord{\partial\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}})\sqcup_{\underline{\mathord{\partial\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}}}\underline{\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}\to UL\underline{\mathord{\Delta}_{+}[n]}

is a trivial cofibration.
It then follows from
Lemma

3.20
that the same holds for all cofibrations and
the case of
{\mathord{\varnothing}}\to X
concludes the proof.
Thus it suffices to prove the statement in the case of semisimplicial sets which is
[
23
]
*Proposition 5.5.14.
∎

are detected
by pointwise evaluation.

For a general morphism
X\to Y
, we consider its fibrant replacement as constructed in the small object argument.
Since
U
preserves trivial cofibrations (by
Lemma

12.12
) and fibrations, it follows that it preserves such fibrant replacements.
Thus the conclusion follows from the special case of morphisms between fibrant objects.
∎

12.16
.

For each
X\in\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
, the counit
LUX\to X
is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

This follows from the triangle identities using
Lemmas

12.14
and
12.15
.
∎

.

is countably lextensive, the functor

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}_{\mathsf{fib}}
is an equivalence
of fibration categories.

Proof.

Consider the functor
L^{\prime}\colon{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}_{\mathsf{fib}}\to\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
obtained by composing
L
with
a chosen fibrant replacement functor in
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
.
Such fibrant replacement along with the unit of the adjunction
L\dashv U
induce a natural transformation

\operatorname{id}_{{\text{\hbox to6.24pt{\vbox to3.11pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 1.34166pt\lower-1.55554pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.34166pt}{-1.55554pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

{}{}}}{
{}{}}

\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}_{\mathsf{fib}}}\to UL^{\prime}
which is a weak equivalence by
Lemmas

12.14
and
12.15
.
Similarly, using the counit we obtain two natural transformations
L^{\prime}UX\leftarrow LUX\to X
for
X\in\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
.
They are weak equivalences by definition and by
Corollary

12.16
, but
LU
is not an endofunctor of
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
,
just of
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
.
However, we can apply a functorial factorisation to the morphism
LUX\to L^{\prime}UX\times X
to obtain
a weak equivalence
LUX\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.458pt\hskip-6.458pt\hbox{$\xrightarrow{\makebox[6.3778pt]{}}$}\hskip-6.458pt\hskip-3.3436pt\raisebox{5.14444pt}{\hbox{${\mathrel{\sim}}$}}\hskip-3.3436pt\hskip 6.458pt}}TX
and a fibration
TX\twoheadrightarrow L^{\prime}UX\times X
.
Then
T
is an endofunctor of
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
and we have
two natural weak equivalences
L^{\prime}U\leftarrow T\to\operatorname{id}_{\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}}
as required.
∎

12.18
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a countably lextensive category. Then the effective model structure on
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
is left proper.

Proof.

This follows by the combination of the following facts. First, the functor
LU\colon\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}\to\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
preserves colimits;
secondly,
LU
preserves cofibrations by
Lemmas

12.11
and
12.13
;
thirdly,
LU
takes values in cofibrant objects by
Lemmas

12.3
and
12.11
;
and, finally, the counit
LUX\to X
is a weak equivalence by
Corollary

12.16
.
∎

13
The
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
.

Section

11
provides a description of the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
presented by the effective model structure on
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}

when
\mathcal{E}
is completely lextensive and locally connected.
The goal of this section is to give an alternative characterisation of this
\infty
-category under fewer assumptions on
\mathcal{E}
.
As shown in
Section

1
, if
\mathcal{E}
is only a category with finite limits, we already have a fibration category structure on
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
,
which, in the case where
\mathcal{E}
is countably lextensive corresponds to the category of fibrant objects of the effective model structure
hence models the same
\infty
-category.
We will consider the more general problem of describing the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
in this case.

We do not know such description for a general category
\mathcal{E}
with finite limits, but we will present an answer that applies when
\mathcal{E}
is
either countably complete or countably lextensive.
More precisely, we will give a description of the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.91666pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{
{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}_{\mathsf{fib}}
,
which we showed in
Section

12
is equivalent to
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
when
\mathcal{E}
is either countably lextensive or countably complete.

13.1
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a category that is either countably complete or countably lextensive.
Then, evaluations at all
E\in\mathcal{E}
induce a fully faithful embedding of
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
into the category of presheaves of spaces over
\mathcal{E}
.
More precisely,
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
is equivalent to the full subcategory of presheaves of spaces over
\mathcal{E}

that are homotopy colimits (geometric realisations) of Kan complexes in
\mathcal{E}
.

This is closely related to the exact completion (or ex/lex completion) of
\mathcal{E}
.
In general, the exact completion (see, e.g.,
[
5
]
) of a category
\mathcal{E}
with finite limits can be described as
the full subcategory of
\operatorname{\mathsf{Psh}}\mathcal{E}
of objects that can be written as colimits of “setoids objects” in
\mathcal{E}
, i.e., as coequalisers of “proof-relevant equivalence relations”, that is diagrams
R\rightrightarrows X
in
\mathcal{E}
, such that the image of the map

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,R)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,X)\times\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,X)

is an equivalence relation on
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,X)
for each
E\in\mathcal{E}
.
The term “proof-relevant” refers to the fact that we do not assume that
R\to X\times X
is a monomorphism, or equivalently that
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,R)
is a subset of
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,X)\times\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{Set}}(E,X)
.
The fact that
R\to X\times X
is a proof-relevant equivalence relation can be encoded as a structure consisting of morphisms in
\mathcal{E}
witnessing transitivity (
R\times_{X}R\to R
), symmetry (
R\to R
) and reflexivity (
X\to R
).
Proposition

1.4
can be seen as a higher categorical version of this observation, i.e.,
Kan simplicial objects are a higher categorical generalisation of proof-relevant equivalence relations.
In fact, it is easy to deduce from the theorem above that the full subcategory of set-truncated objects in
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
is equivalent to
the ex/lex completion of
\mathcal{E}
.

However, it does not seem accurate to think of
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
as the
\infty
-categorical version of the ex/lex completion.
Let us say that an
\infty
-category is exact if it has finite limits and quotients of groupoid objects exist and are van Kampen colimits.
Lurie has shown that this condition together with complete lextensivity and local presentability characterises
\infty
-toposes
[
35
]
.
We can then define the ex/lex completion of an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
with finite limits in the usual way:
it is an exact
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}^{\text{ex/lex}}
with a functor
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{C}^{\mathrm{ex/lex}}
such that
any finite limit preserving functor to an exact
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{D}
extends essentially uniquely to
an exact functor
\mathcal{C}^{\text{ex/lex}}\to\mathcal{D}
.
We conjecture that the effective model structure is related to this ex/lex completion operation in the following way:

13.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a countably lextensive category or countably complete category. The ex/lex completion of the
\infty
-category associated to
\mathcal{E}
is equivalent to the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
on objects that are
n
-truncated for some
n
.

More generally, we believe that this holds for any finitely complete category
\mathcal{E}
when
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}})
is replaced with
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}({\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-1.91666pt}{-2.22221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}}{}{{}}{}
{
{}}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
{
{}}{{}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{0.5}{0.0}{0.0}{0.5}{1.68056pt}{-4.47221pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{
{}{}{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\mathcal{E}}_{\mathsf{fib}})
.

The general idea of the proof of
Theorem

13.1
is that the category
\operatorname{\mathsf{Fam}}\mathcal{E}
of families of objects of
\mathcal{E}
is always
a completely lextensive locally connected category, such that
\mathcal{E}
can be identified with its category of connected objects.
Hence, we can apply
Theorem

11.7
to it and show that

\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E})\simeq\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}\operatorname{\mathsf{sPsh}}\mathcal{E}

The right hand side is a model for the
\infty
-category of small presheaves of spaces on
\mathcal{E}
(in the
\infty
-categorical sense).
We always have a fully faithful embedding
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}\to\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E}
which identifies
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
with the full subcategory of levelwise connected simplicial objects.
Moreover, a map is a fibration or a weak equivalence (between fibrant objects) in
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
if and only if its image in
\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E}
is one,
so this embedding also restricts to a morphism of fibration categories.

Our goal is to show that (under the assumptions of
Theorem

13.1
) this also induces a fully faithful embedding on the level of the
\infty
-categories.
Unfortunately, we are able to give a proof of this only when we consider instead the semisimplicial version of this embedding
{\text{\hbox to7.39pt{\vbox to4.44pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\thinspace\lower-2.22221pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{

{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

Because of the following lemma, it is enough to prove that it is fully faithful at the level of the homotopy categories.

13.3
.

A finite limit preserving functor between two
\infty
-categories which is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful) on the homotopy categories is an equivalence (resp. fully faithful).

Proof.

This is shown for the case of equivalences in
[
10
]
*Theorem 7.6.10.
The case of fully faithful functors can be deduced from the case of equivalences.
Let
f\colon\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a finite limit preserving functor which is fully faithful on the homotopy category and let
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
denote its essential image.
Then
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
contains the terminal object since
f
preserves finite limits.
Similarly,
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
is closed under pullbacks.
Indeed, since
f
is fully faithful on the homotopy categories, any cospan in
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
can be lifted to a cospan in
\mathcal{X}
.
Its pullback exists in
\mathcal{X}
and is preserved by
f
.
It follows that
f
induces a finite limit preserving functor
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
which is fully faithful and essentially surjective on the homotopy categories,
so it is an equivalence, and hence by the result above,
f
induces an equivalence between
\mathcal{X}
and
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}
, i.e., it is fully faithful.
∎

13.5
.

.
However, a non-constant simplicial object in
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}
is levelwise connected in

and thus not cofibrant by

4.6
.

We are now ready to prove
Theorem

13.1
.

.

Theorem

12.17
shows that the bottom horizontal functor is always an equivalence of the homotopy categories
as
\operatorname{\mathsf{Fam}}\mathcal{C}
is always a completely lextensive category.
The top horizontal map is also an equivalence on the homotopy categories by
Theorem

12.6
since
\mathcal{E}
is countably lextensive or countably complete.
Finally, we have shown in
Theorem

13.4
that the right vertical functor is fully faithful on the homotopy categories.
It follows that the left vertical functor is also fully faithful on the homotopy categories, and hence by
Lemma

13.3
induces
a fully faithful embedding of
\infty
-categories
\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}\to(\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E})_{\mathsf{fib}}
.

Now,
\operatorname{\mathsf{Fam}}\mathcal{E}
is a locally connected completely lextensive category, and
\mathcal{E}
is its category of connected objects.
Hence, by
Theorem

11.7
, the
\infty
-category
\operatorname{Ho_{\infty}}(\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E})_{\mathsf{fib}}
is equivalent to the category of presheaves of spaces over
\mathcal{E}
,
which proves the first half of the theorem.

For the description of the essential image we simply investigate the precise nature of the embedding constructed above. If
X\in\mathsf{s}\mathcal{E}_{\mathsf{fib}}
then its image in
(\operatorname{\mathsf{sFam}}\mathcal{E})_{\mathsf{fib}}
is also fibrant, and the objects corresponding to
E\in\mathcal{E}
are cofibrant, so, as this is a simplicial model category, the Hom space in the corresponding
\infty
-category between them is simply
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{sSet}}(E,X)
. Hence
X
is sent to the presheaf of spaces
E\mapsto\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathsf{sSet}}(E,X)
. Note that as colimits in presheaf categories are computed levelwise and the colimit of a simplicial set in the
\infty
-category of spaces is the the spaces represented by this simplicial sets, this can equivalently be expressed as the fact that
X
is sent to its geometric realisation in the presheaf category.
∎

Appendix Appendix A
Remarks on constructivity

While the present paper has been written within ZFC for simplicity, many of our results
and proofs are constructive, i.e., do not rely on the law of excluded middle or the axiom of choice,
subject to some clarifications, which we will discuss briefly here.

First of all, in the constructive reading of the paper, a
finite set
means a finite cardinal, or a finite decidable set, i.e., a set equipped with a bijection with
\{1,\dots,n\}
, for some
n\in\mathbb{N}
. A
countable set
is a set which is equipped with a bijection with either
\{1,\dots,n\}
or
\mathbb{N}
. With this definition, a countable coproduct of countable sets is countable.

Secondly, we restrict ourselves to consider finitely lextensive, countably lextensive and completely lextensive categories. Here,
by a
finitely lextensive
category we mean a category with a strict initial object and van Kampen binary coproducts and by a
countably lextensive
category we mean a finitely lextensive category that in addition has
\mathbb{N}
-indexed van Kampen coproducts. With this definition, the category of countable sets is countably lextensive. Without these changes, we would run into problems as
\omega_{1}
is not a regular cardinal in ZF and the axiom of countable choice is needed to show that a countable union of countable set is countable, and therefore
Definition

2.4
would be problematic, as we could not show that the category of countable
sets is countably lextensive.

Finally, one should assume the convention that every time we discuss existence of an object, this involves explicit structure, rather than a mere property. For example, when we say that a map
f
has the left lifting property against
g
, we mean that
f
comes equipped with a function that associates a solution to each lifting problem.

We make no claim on whether it is possible to make the results in
Section

11
and
Section

13
constructive. Indeed, both of these sections involve
\infty
-categories, for which a constructive theory has not been developed yet. Also,
Lemma

11.3
is non-constructive: in a constructive setting, its conclusion should be taken as the definition of a connected object. Finally,
Section

11
relies on the existence of the projective model structure on the category of small presheaves on a large category, which is not known to exist constructively.
